## CONSTRAINT:
**You must only answer ONE PAIR.**

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:
Please follow this format:

**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

---

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, followed by nothing else.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Andrews states that she was the one who knocked the flower vase over, where it fell onto the guitar case (Testimony 5). However, Evidence 9 (Crime Photo) describes the scene where the remains of a smashed vase stained the carpet with water, and the guitar case lies on the floor next to the victim, but only the top of the lid has water on it.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us understand the contradiction better. Proposition 3 states that if the Vase blocks the view of Corrida, then Corrida cannot be seen. This proposition does not directly address the contradiction regarding the placement of water on the guitar case.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

None of the spatial propositions directly address the contradiction between Andrews' testimony and the Crime Photo evidence.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises from the spatial arrangement of the objects described in the Crime Photo and Andrews' testimony. According to Andrews, she knocked over the vase, causing it to fall onto the guitar case. However, the Crime Photo indicates that the water from the vase only stained the carpet and the top of the guitar case's lid, suggesting that the vase did not fall directly onto the guitar case but rather beside it or in such a way that only the top lid was affected.

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

{"evidence": 9, "testimony": 5}

--------------------
{
  "evidence": 9,
  "testimony": 5

Ini Miney's testimony places her in the passenger's seat and specifically on the right side of the car, while Mimi Miney, as the driver, would be on the left side. This arrangement makes it impossible for Ini Miney to be covered by Mimi Miney, thus creating a contradiction with Prop 3.

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, followed by nothing else.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Agent Lang states that the president went up to the roof with Judge Gavèlle (Testimony 0), and that Judge Gavèlle came back down on the elevator alone (Testimony 1). This implies that President Wang did not come down via the elevator. Testimony 3 further supports this by stating there's no other way for the president to get down without using the elevator, suggesting that if he didn't use the elevator, he could not have left the roof.

However, Evidence 10 shows security camera footage of President Wang and Judge Gavèlle on the roof of the Bigg Building, but it also notes that there's no footage of the president riding the elevator down to leave. This suggests that President Wang was indeed on the roof but did not use the elevator to leave, which contradicts the implication in Lang's testimonies that the president couldn't have left the roof without using the elevator.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

None of the spatial propositions directly address the issue of how President Wang left the roof or whether he could leave without using the elevator. However, we can infer a contradiction based on the spatial arrangement and the logical implications of the testimonies and evidence.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Since no spatial propositions directly apply, we move to step 4.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises from the spatial arrangement and the logical flow of events. According to Lang's testimonies, the only way for President Wang to leave the roof is via the elevator, yet the security camera footage (Evidence 10) shows that President Wang was on the roof but does not show him leaving via the elevator. This implies that either President Wang left the roof in another way, or he never left the roof, which contradicts the testimonies suggesting he had to leave via the elevator.

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

{"evidence": 10, "testimony": 3}

{
  "evidence": 10,
  "testimony": 3

**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Kay mentions seeing something red from under the cherry tree (Testimony 3) and someone walking towards her from the other side of the candy stand (Testimony 4). These statements suggest specific locations relative to the cherry tree and the candy stand.

From the evidences, Evidence 7 (Bigg Building Pamphlet) provides a layout of the viewing platform where the cherry tree is located on the left-most edge and the candy stand is on the right-most edge. 

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions relate to the positions of the cherry tree and the candy stand or the visibility of objects from these positions.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Propositions 1, 2, and 8 involve the Red Raincoat and its position relative to the cherry tree and the candy stand. Proposition 8 specifically states that if the Jammin' Ninja Mask is covered by the Red Raincoat and the Red Raincoat is to the left of the Candy Stand, and the Victim's Letter is covered by the Candy Stand, then the Jammin' Ninja Mask is to the left of the Victim's Letter. This proposition does not directly address the contradiction but helps us understand the relative positioning of objects.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

None of the propositions directly address the contradiction between Kay's testimonies and the layout described in the Bigg Building Pamphlet. However, we can infer a contradiction based on the spatial descriptions given. According to Testimony 3, Kay sees something red from under the cherry tree, which is on the left-most edge. According to Testimony 4, someone walks towards her from the other side of the candy stand, which is on the right-most edge. Given the layout, it would be contradictory if Kay could see something red from under the cherry tree while simultaneously having someone walk towards her from the other side of the candy stand, unless she moved or the red object was visible from both sides.

Additionally, there are some pieces of paper near the coffee table. However, the Crime Photo does not specify the exact position of the Sports Paper relative to the victim's seat.

However, Evidence 11, the Meraktis's Autopsy Report, indicates that the cause of death was a single bullet to the right temple. This suggests that the bullet hit from the side rather than the front.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

Among the spatial propositions provided, Proposition 1 is relevant because it deals with the direction of the bullet and the resulting fall of the victim.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 states: "If the bullet hit Pal Meraktis from the front then Pal Meraktis falls backward."

This proposition implies that if Stickler's testimony about the bullet hitting from the front is true, then Pal Meraktis would have fallen backward. However, the autopsy report contradicts Stickler's testimony by specifying the bullet hit the right temple, indicating a different trajectory and potentially a different fall direction.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 1 does apply and helps us identify the contradiction. Stickler's testimony about the bullet hitting from the front conflicts with the autopsy report stating the bullet hit the right temple, suggesting the bullet came from a different angle.

**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Ema states that the victim (Dumas Gloomsbury) was hit from behind and fell over into the lantern. This suggests that the victim was initially positioned such that the attacker was behind him, and after being hit, he fell forward into the lantern.

However, Evidence 6, the Photo of Victim, shows the victim lying face up inside the lantern. This implies that the victim did not fall forward into the lantern but rather was placed or positioned in such a way that he ended up face up.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us understand the contradiction better. Proposition 1 is particularly relevant here because it deals with the direction of the fall based on the direction of the attack.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 states: "If Dumas Gloomsbury is hit from behind then Dumas Gloomsbury falls forward." According to Ema's testimony, Dumas Gloomsbury was hit from behind, which should have caused him to fall forward. However, the photo of the victim shows him lying face up, which contradicts the expected outcome of falling forward.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 1 does apply and helps us identify the contradiction. The spatial reasoning is that if someone is hit from behind, they would typically fall forward unless they were deliberately positioned face up after the attack.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

We need to look for inconsistencies in the spatial descriptions provided by the testimonies and the evidences. Specifically, we will focus on the location of the victim, Clay Terran, and the path he and the defendant, Starbuck, took during the evacuation.

From the testimonies:
- Testimony 0: Starbuck states that he supported Clay over his shoulder and got them out of the rocket.
- Testimony 1: Starbuck mentions taking the elevator down to the middle level and heading for the corridor.
- Testimony 2: Starbuck says Clay had passed out by the time they got the order to evacuate.
- Testimony 3: Starbuck claims he was trying to save Clay.

From the evidences:
- Evidence 2: The Space Center Diagram shows a passage connecting the mid level of Launch Pad 1 to the 3rd-floor lounge of the main building.
- Evidence 7: The Security Camera Video shows the defendant and victim escaping from the corridor to the boarding lounge.
- Evidence 1: Terran's Autopsy Report indicates that the victim was discovered in the third-floor boarding lounge in the main building.
- Evidence 10: The Bullet Hole found in the boarding lounge suggests that the bullet came from outside the boarding lounge.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions help us identify a contradiction. Propositions 1, 2, 4, and 5 are related to the location and direction of the bullet and the fall of the victim, while Proposition 7 is about the connection between the launch pad and the boarding lounge.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 7 states: "If the Space Center Diagram shows the passage between the mid level of Launch Pad 1 and the 3rd-floor lounge of the main building then the passage connects these two areas."

This proposition supports the idea that there is a direct passage between the mid level of Launch Pad 1 and the 3rd-floor lounge of the main building. However, it does not directly address the contradiction.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises from the fact that Starbuck's testimonies suggest that he and Clay escaped from the rocket via the corridor and then entered the boarding lounge. However, the bullet hole found in the boarding lounge (Evidence 10) and the autopsy report (Evidence 1) indicate that Clay was shot and fell in the boarding lounge, suggesting he was already inside the lounge when he was shot.

Starbuck's testimonies imply that he was carrying Clay out of the rocket and into the boarding lounge, but the presence of the bullet hole and the autopsy report suggest that Clay was shot while already inside the boarding lounge, which contradicts Starbuck's claim of supporting Clay out of the rocket.

However, Evidence 4 (Study Diagram) indicates that the bookshelf is on the other side of the window, suggesting that Dr. Buff would be behind the window relative to the bookshelf.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions relate to the positioning of Dr. Buff relative to the window and the bookshelf.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

None of the spatial propositions directly address the specific spatial relationship between Dr. Buff, the window, and the bookshelf. However, we can use logical reasoning based on the descriptions provided.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Based on Atishon's testimony, Dr. Buff was standing in front of the bookshelves, which suggests that the bookshelves were behind Dr. Buff relative to the window. But according to the Study Diagram (Evidence 4), the bookshelf is located on the other side of the window, meaning Dr. Buff would be in front of the window and thus in front of the bookshelf from Atishon's perspective outside the window. This creates a contradiction because Atishon's description of Dr. Buff's position does not align with the layout described in the Study Diagram.

However, Evidence 12 (Broken Prop Sword) suggests that the sword was used to jam the handle to the hideout's door from the inside, indicating that the door was indeed locked and could not be passed through.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 4 states: "If the door leading outside the hideout is locked then the door leading outside the hideout cannot be passed through."

This proposition supports Lance Amano's claim that the door leading outside the hideout was locked and thus could not be passed through. However, it does not address the existence or accessibility of the underground passageway.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

While Proposition 4 confirms that the door leading outside the hideout was locked, it does not provide information about the underground passageway. The contradiction arises from the fact that Lance Amano's testimony implies that the underground passageway was accessible and used for his escape, whereas the presence of the Broken Prop Sword suggests that the only exit (the door leading outside) was blocked, leaving no clear indication of how he could have used the underground passageway if it was also part of the same hideout structure.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

However, Evidence 29 indicates that the Yatagarasu escaped through the Revolving Fireplace Wall, which means the Yatagarasu was not present to block Shih-na's view.

- **Proposition 3:** If Oliver Deacon was shot from the lower right abdomen through the right shoulder then he fell backward.
